# Subject Benchmark Statement: Agriculture, Rural Environmental Sciences, Animal Studies, Consumer Science, Forestry, Food, Horticulture and Human Nutrition (QAA, April 2024)

> Run 0083, P1 by std. The issuer's words are held in the source library, `wkspc2601-library/txt/qaa-2024-agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-standard.norm.txt`; each S4 record carries its statement in full (`source`) for language analysis, with its span. Paraphrases and versions are ours. Private to the project, never published. In front matter `status` is the issuer's; ours is `lifecycle`. Doubts are in Part E.

## Part 0 Summary

### 0.1 Agent definition
<!-- label: editorial; status: drafted; basis: primary -->
QAA's UK reference point for what an honours graduate should know, understand and be able to do across a family of land-based and food subjects: agriculture, horticulture and forestry; rural environmental sciences; animal studies (non-production animals); and food science, food technology, human nutrition and consumer science. Reach for it when designing, approving, reviewing or describing an honours course or its outcomes in any of these areas, or when placing a student's work against the threshold, typical and excellent rungs. It is a sector reference point, not a curriculum, a regulation or a professional body's accreditation standard.

### 0.2 Summary
<!-- label: both; status: drafted; basis: primary -->
Published by QAA in April 2024 as the fifth edition, the statement covers four subject groupings, each a subject in its own right. It sets out the context and purposes of the subjects, with the cross-cutting themes of equality, diversity and inclusion, accessibility, sustainability (education for sustainable development) and enterprise and entrepreneurship education, plus a section on generative AI; the distinctive features of course design, progression, flexibility, partnership and review; content, teaching and assessment; and the benchmark standards. The standards are six subject tables (agriculture, horticulture, forestry, rural environmental sciences, animal studies, and food science, technology, nutrition and consumer science), 76 outcomes in all, each read at three rungs by depth: familiarity and awareness at threshold, well-founded understanding at typical, deep and full understanding at excellent. Nineteen generic skills in five groups follow. It covers bachelor's degrees with honours only and points to Annex D of the qualifications frameworks for degree classes. It names professional and accrediting bodies in general terms and cites the QAA and Advance HE ESD guidance and several neighbouring benchmark statements.

- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/1.15
  locator: { number: "1.15", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of an Agriculture, Rural Environmental Sciences, Animal Studies, Consumer Science, Forestry, Food, Horticulture and Human Nutrition Degree", "Sustainability"] }
  span: { start: 16702, end: 17131 }
  source: "1.15 The application of sustainable development in the higher education sector involves creating curriculum structures and subject-relevant content to support and enact sustainable development. To support the inclusion of sustainable principles within these disciplines, providers should refer to the QAA Education for Sustainable Development Guidance for key competencies, learning outcomes and assessment within the curricula.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "providers"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["refer"]
  about: "using the ESD guidance"
  themes: [theme:esd]
  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Sustainable development in higher education means building curriculum structures and content that support it; to include sustainable principles, providers should consult the QAA ESD guidance on key competencies, outcomes and assessment."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/1.23
  locator: { number: "1.23", headingPath: ["Context and purposes of an Agriculture, Rural Environmental Sciences, Animal Studies, Consumer Science, Forestry, Food, Horticulture and Human Nutrition Degree", "Generative AI"] }
  span: { start: 20174, end: 20617 }
  source: "1.23 Generative AI (artificial intelligence) and other technologies should be analysed in terms of their impact on the agri-food supply chain and also used with critical reflection as tools for a range of purposes. Transparency and openness in the use of these technologies will enable judgements to be made on their appropriate use. Graduates should recognise positive opportunities for use of AI but equally the need for responsible use.\n\n7\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "courses and graduates in these subjects"
  addressee: both
  verbs: ["analysed", "used", "recognise"]
  about: "generative AI and its responsible use"
  themes: [theme:generative-ai]
  domain: generic-skill
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Generative AI and related technologies should be examined for their effect on the agri-food supply chain and used, with critical reflection, for varied purposes; being open about their use allows judgements on what is appropriate, and graduates should see both the opportunities and the need for responsibility."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/2.1
  locator: { number: "2.1", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of an Agriculture, Rural Environmental Sciences, Animal Studies, Consumer Science, Forestry, Food, Horticulture and Human Nutrition Degree", "Design"] }
  span: { start: 20786, end: 21315 }
  source: "2.1 Courses, components and individual learning experiences should be inclusive in their design and approach. Providers make use of frameworks such as Universal Design for Learning (UDL). Course leaders consider the profile of their students, both potential and actual, to ensure that programme learning outcomes are inclusive. Opportunities to develop graduate skills and attributes are embedded throughout the learning journey, with teaching approaches being firmly student-centred to enable an inclusive learning environment.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "courses, components and learning experiences"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["ensure", "embedded"]
  about: "inclusive course design"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Courses, their parts and each learning experience should be designed inclusively; providers draw on frameworks such as Universal Design for Learning, leaders consider their students' profiles so outcomes are inclusive, and graduate skills are built in throughout with student-centred teaching."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/2.5
  locator: { number: "2.5", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of an Agriculture, Rural Environmental Sciences, Animal Studies, Consumer Science, Forestry, Food, Horticulture and Human Nutrition Degree", "Design"] }
  span: { start: 22594, end: 23562 }
  source: "2.5 Horticulture courses mainly focus on developing knowledge of plant biology, physiology, pathology and soil structure, the application of horticultural practices, the management of plant production systems, the management and maintenance of a variety of landscapes. The aim is to take that knowledge and meet social, financial, nutritional and environmental needs, and the underpinning scientific basis of plant breeding and future crops (including technologies). The courses have a theme of sustainability and responsible use of resources throughout. Many of the topics discussed explore social, cultural, ethical, equitable, financial and future themes for national and international contexts. The courses may have a greater emphasis on practical or theoretical skills. Graduates will be equipped to advise on and apply skills to industry standards in disciplines, including production horticulture, landscape management, amenity horticulture and research roles.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: will
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "horticulture graduates"
  addressee: learner
  verbs: ["equipped", "advise", "apply"]
  about: "what horticulture graduates are ready for"
  themes: [none]
  domain: subject-skill
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Horticulture courses cover plant biology and soils, horticultural practice and the management of production systems and landscapes, with sustainability throughout; graduates will be ready to advise and to apply their skills to industry standards in production, landscape and amenity horticulture and research."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/2.11
  locator: { number: "2.11", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of an Agriculture, Rural Environmental Sciences, Animal Studies, Consumer Science, Forestry, Food, Horticulture and Human Nutrition Degree", "Progression"] }
  span: { start: 26778, end: 27714 }
  source: "2.11 During undergraduate study students will progress from one level of study to the next, in line with the regulations and processes for each institution. However, it is expected that each level would see the attainment of knowledge, analytical skills, expertise and experience that builds towards the final achievement of meeting the threshold-level subject-specific and generic skills listed in this Statement. This will usually include successful completion and the award of credit for the full range of learning and assessment, including any practical and work-related components. Upon graduation, it is expected that a student who has achieved a second-class degree or higher would be equipped for undertaking postgraduate study in this or a related discipline. Entry requirements to postgraduate courses are, however, determined by individual providers and may require specified levels of achievement at undergraduate level.\n\n9\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: will
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "undergraduates in these subjects"
  addressee: learner
  verbs: ["progress", "include"]
  about: "progression towards the threshold"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Students will move from level to level under their institution's rules, each level building towards the threshold subject-specific and generic skills; this will usually mean completing and gaining credit for all learning and assessment, practical and work-related parts included; a second-class degree or better is expected to prepare a graduate for postgraduate study, though each provider sets its own entry rules."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/2.12
  locator: { number: "2.12", headingPath: ["Distinctive features of an Agriculture, Rural Environmental Sciences, Animal Studies, Consumer Science, Forestry, Food, Horticulture and Human Nutrition Degree", "Progression"] }
  span: { start: 27714, end: 28143 }
  source: "2.12 Undergraduates studying these courses as part of a combined or joint degree with other subjects will achieve core elements of the specific and generic skills outlined in this Statement and will add others according to the topics covered in the other subject(s) of their degree. Additionally, they may explore the overlap between different disciplines, creating further opportunities for interdisciplinary study. Flexibility\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: will
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "students on joint or combined degrees"
  addressee: learner
  verbs: ["achieve", "add"]
  about: "joint and combined degrees"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Students taking these subjects in a joint or combined degree will reach the core subject and generic skills and add others from their other subject, with room for interdisciplinary study where the disciplines overlap."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: both
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/2.14

  span: { start: 28657, end: 28950 }
  source: "2.14 Co-creation and co-delivery in teaching and learning should be considered as a mechanism of harnessing stakeholder engagement to enhance courses based on the ideas of prospective students, current students, graduates, course teams, degree accreditors and employers.   source: "2.16 Externality is an essential component of the quality assurance system in the UK.   span: { start: 32631, end: 33050 }
  source: "3.4 Courses in this subject will include the knowledge, understanding, skills and expertise described in section 4. - id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/3.6
  locator: { number: "3.6", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Content"] }
  span: { start: 33292, end: 33876 }
  source: "3.6 Courses will include the knowledge, understanding, skills and expertise described in section 4. Due to the nature of this subject area, readers may find it useful to refer to more than one Subject Benchmark Statement. For example, those with an interest in equine sports coaching may find the Subject Benchmark Statement for Events, Hospitality, Leisure, Sport and Tourism of interest. Similarly, readers with an interest in bioveterinary science may find the Subject Benchmark Statement for Biosciences useful. Food Science, Food Technology, Human Nutrition and Consumer Science\n"

  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: will
  level: none
  appliesTo: "animal studies courses"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["include"]
  about: "animal studies courses cover section 4"

  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Animal studies courses will cover the knowledge, understanding, skills and expertise in section 4; readers may also find other statements useful, such as events, hospitality, leisure, sport and tourism for equine sports coaching and biosciences for bioveterinary science."

- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/3.14
  locator: { number: "3.14", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Teaching and learning"] }
  span: { start: 37822, end: 38107 }
  source: "3.14 This Subject Benchmark Statement does not specify which methods of teaching or learning should be employed in relevant courses; however, learning outcomes should be achievable through the methods utilised, taking into account any requirements of professional bodies, if required.\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none

  level: none
  appliesTo: "courses in these subjects"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["taking"]

  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "The statement does not prescribe teaching or learning methods, but learning outcomes should be reachable through the methods chosen, allowing for any professional body requirements."

- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/3.17
  locator: { number: "3.17", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Teaching and learning"] }
  span: { start: 39571, end: 40380 }
  source: "3.17 Advances in knowledge and understanding in these subject areas are founded on accurate observation and recording in the field, and the collection of empirical data for analysis. Developing practical and research skills is therefore important for students wishing to pursue careers in related sectors. Practical studies allow students to develop and enhance many graduate attributes and transferable skills. These include leadership, team working, problem- solving, self-management and interpersonal relationships, which are of value to their employability, entrepreneurship and active citizenship. Activities are designed to be inclusive to all students and, where required, reasonable adjustments should be made to enable all students to take part in practical work and fieldwork activities. Assessment\n"
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: none
  appliesTo: "practical and fieldwork activities"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["made", "enable"]
  about: "adjustments for practical work and fieldwork"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Progress in these subjects rests on careful field observation and empirical data, so practical and research skills matter; practical work builds attributes such as leadership, teamwork and problem-solving; activities are designed to be inclusive, and reasonable adjustments should be made so all students can do practical work and fieldwork."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/3.19
  locator: { number: "3.19", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Assessment"] }
  span: { start: 41123, end: 41317 }
  source: "3.19 Providers must take every opportunity to promote the voice of students, including the co- design of assessment. To improve employability, assessment should be authentic to post-study life.\n"

  statementType: requirement
  rung: none
  force: must
  level: none
  appliesTo: "providers"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["promote"]
  about: "student voice in assessment; authentic assessment"

  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Providers must take every chance to promote the student voice, including co-designing assessment; to help employability, assessment should reflect life after study."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited
- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/3.21
  locator: { number: "3.21", headingPath: ["Content, structure and delivery", "Assessment"] }
  span: { start: 42066, end: 42429 }
  source: "3.21 Assessment should be inclusive for all students. It should be designed in a way that facilitates learning and supports students to succeed, and it is clearly linked to the specified learning outcomes or desired competencies. Where appropriate, modified and alternative provision with reasonable adjustments should be available to avoid barriers to learning.\n"

- id: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2
  locator: { number: "4.2", headingPath: ["Benchmark standards", "Introduction; Subject knowledge, understanding and skills"] }
  span: { start: 44588, end: 45068 }
  source: "The vast majority of students will perform significantly better than the minimum threshold standards. Each higher education provider has its own method of determining what appropriate evidence of this achievement will be and should refer to Annex D in The Frameworks for Higher Education Qualifications of UK Degree-Awarding Bodies. This Annex sets out common descriptions of the four main degree outcome classifications for bachelor's degrees with honours: 1st, 2.1, 2.2 and 3rd."
  stem: none
  statementType: expectation
  rung: none
  force: should
  level: fheq:6
  appliesTo: "providers judging achievement"
  addressee: provider
  verbs: ["perform", "refer"]
  about: "evidence of achievement and Annex D"
  themes: [none]
  domain: none
  award: ["bachelor's with honours"]
  gradeOf: none
  paraphrase: "Most students will do much better than the minimum threshold; each provider decides what counts as evidence of achievement and should refer to Annex D of the qualifications frameworks, which describes the four honours classes."
  quote: full   # the issuer's statement is in `source` (D-std-0014)
  audience: practitioner
  subjects: inherited

- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/1.5

  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a should-level expectation on the course team and institution, not a student outcome: EDI built into each stage of the student journey. It holds for honours courses in all four groupings. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.5. Do not present it as a legal duty; set it beside 1.6, 1.10, 2.1 and 3.21 in this document."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a programme team reviewing recruitment, induction, teaching or careers support. Frame it for staff as an expectation that inclusion reaches every stage, not one module. Ask: 'At which stage of the student journey is inclusion weakest in your course, and what evidence shows it?' Do not hand this to a student as something they must achieve."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/1.6
  forPractitioner: { text: "Make sure bursaries, scholarships and similar support are accessible and actively publicised to applicants and current students, alongside a safe, inclusive environment.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a should-level expectation on the provider: inclusive environments, with financial support made accessible and publicised. It is about the institution, not a level of study. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.6. Do not phrase it as a student outcome; set it beside 1.5 and 1.7 to 1.9 (the plain-present EDI commitments)."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a team or a student services colleague asks how financial and inclusion support should reach students. Avoid presenting it to a student as their responsibility."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/1.10
  forPractitioner: { text: "Look at what students learn implicitly about the discipline's norms and values; where some students reach that knowledge later or less fully than others, it becomes a barrier to address.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a should-level expectation on course teams to consider the hidden curriculum as a barrier to success. It applies to honours courses in these subjects.   author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a team reviewing why some groups of students do less well, or planning induction. For staff, put it as the unwritten expectations that some students learn late. - statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/1.14
  forPractitioner: { text: "Treat sustainability as holistic when building curricula and modules, and, where it fits, weave the SDGs through the whole curriculum rather than one unit.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }

  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a team maps sustainability content or plans a curriculum review. Frame it for staff as integration across the course, not a single sustainability module. - statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/1.15
  forPractitioner: { text: "When building sustainability into outcomes and assessment, use the QAA and Advance HE ESD guidance on key competencies, learning outcomes and assessment.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }

  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a team writes outcomes or assessments on sustainability and asks where to start. Point staff to the ESD guidance's competencies as the reference. - statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/1.23
  forPractitioner: { text: "Teach students to analyse what generative AI does to the agri-food supply chain and to use it reflectively and transparently; graduates should recognise both its benefits and the need for responsible use.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "You should be able to see where AI and similar tools can help, judge their effect on the food and farming supply chain, use them openly and with care and understand why responsible use matters.", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a should-level expectation with two parts: a course duty to analyse and use generative AI critically, and a graduate outcome of recognising its opportunities and the need for responsible use, at honours (FHEQ level 6). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 1.23. Do not present it as an AI policy or academic integrity rule; set it beside generic skill 4.3.b17 here and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b7."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student asks whether or how to use AI tools, or a team plans guidance on AI. To a student, put it plainly: you are expected to use these tools openly and think about their effects; to staff, as both a teaching topic and a practice. Ask: 'Where has an AI tool helped your work, and where would its use be hard to justify?' Avoid treating it as a ban or as permission without limits."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/2.1
  forPractitioner: { text: "Design inclusively from the start, using a framework such as UDL, checking programme outcomes against your actual and likely students, and building graduate skills in throughout.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a should-level expectation on course design: inclusive design of courses and learning experiences, with UDL named as one framework. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.1. Do not claim UDL is required; set it beside 1.5, 2.2 and 3.21 in this document."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a team is designing or revalidating a course. Frame it for staff as design for the whole likely cohort, not adjustments after the event. Ask: 'Who among your students would find your programme outcomes hardest to reach, and why?' Do not present it to students as their task."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/2.5
  forPractitioner: { text: "Prepare horticulture graduates to advise on and apply their skills to industry standard in roles such as production horticulture, landscape management, amenity horticulture and research.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "If you study horticulture, you will be ready to give advice and use your skills to industry standard in work such as growing crops, managing landscapes, amenity horticulture or research.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a will-level expected outcome for horticulture honours graduates (FHEQ level 6): readiness to advise and apply skills to industry standards. Do not claim it guarantees employment or a professional licence; set it beside the horticulture rung items 4.2.b13 to 4.2.b22 and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b9.3."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a horticulture student asks what the degree prepares them for. Put it plainly to a student: you will be able to advise and work to industry standard; to staff, as an outcome their placements and practicals should evidence. Ask: 'Which industry standard have you worked to, and could you advise someone on it?' Avoid promising particular jobs."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/2.11
  forPractitioner: { text: "Build each level towards the threshold skills of section 4; credit for all learning and assessment, practical and work-related parts included, will usually be needed; a 2.2 or better is expected to fit a graduate for postgraduate study.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "As you move through your course, each year builds towards the minimum subject and general skills you need at the end, and you will usually have to complete and gain credit for everything, practical and work-related parts included. With a second-class result or better you can expect to be ready for postgraduate study, though each course sets its own entry rules.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a will-level description of progression: each level builds towards the threshold standards of section 4, with full credit usually required, and a second-class degree expected to equip a graduate for postgraduate study. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 2.11. Do not claim it sets entry rules for postgraduate courses; set it beside 4.1 and 4.2 here and the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b9.3."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student asks whether every module counts or whether their result will let them go on to postgraduate study. To a student, say that each year builds towards the final minimum and that a second-class result usually opens postgraduate study; to staff, frame it as level-by-level design. Ask: 'Which practical or work-based parts have you still to complete for credit?' Avoid promising entry to a particular postgraduate course."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/2.12
  forPractitioner: { text: "On joint or combined degrees, secure the core subject-specific and generic skills, and let the other subject add its own; use the overlap for interdisciplinary work.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "If you study one of these subjects alongside another, you will gain the core skills of this subject and others from your second subject, and you may find chances to work across both.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a will-level expectation for joint and combined honours: the core skills of this statement plus those of the other subject.   author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a joint-honours student or their team asks which standards apply.   forPractitioner: { text: "Choose any teaching methods, provided students can actually reach the learning outcomes through them and any professional body requirements are met.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a should-level expectation that outcomes be achievable through the methods used, with methods left to the course. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.14. Do not claim it prescribes methods; set it beside 3.9, 3.13 and 3.15 in this document."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a team aligns methods with outcomes. Frame it for staff as a check of fit, not a list of required methods. Ask: 'For each outcome, which activity gives students the chance to reach it?' Avoid presenting it to students."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/3.17
  forPractitioner: { text: "Design practical and field activities inclusively and make reasonable adjustments so that every student can take part in practical work and fieldwork.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a should-level expectation on providers: reasonable adjustments so all students can take part in practical work and fieldwork, set in a paragraph on why practical skills matter. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.17. Do not frame it as a student outcome; set it beside 1.11, 1.12 and 3.21 in this document."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a team plans fieldwork or labs, or a student with a disability asks about practical work. Frame it for staff as a duty to adjust, and to a student only as what they can expect. Ask: 'What would stop a student using a wheelchair from doing your field course, and what would you change?' Avoid placing the duty on the student."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/3.19
  forPractitioner: { text: "Give students a real say in assessment, including co-design, as a must; and make assessment authentic to post-study life, as a should.", rewrite: light, force: must, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: must, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a must-level duty on providers to promote the student voice, including assessment co-design, with a should-level expectation of authentic assessment. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.19. Do not weaken the must or strengthen the should; set it beside 3.18, 3.20 and 3.23 in this document."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a team reviews its assessment strategy. Frame it for staff as a firm duty to involve students and an expectation of authentic tasks. Ask: 'Where have students helped design an assessment, and which tasks resemble work they will do after graduating?' Avoid presenting it to a student as their own obligation."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/3.21
  forPractitioner: { text: "Make assessment inclusive and linked to the stated outcomes, and provide modified or alternative arrangements with reasonable adjustments where appropriate.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a should-level expectation on assessment design: inclusive, linked to outcomes, with reasonable adjustments where appropriate. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 3.21. Do not present it as a student outcome; set it beside 1.12, 2.1 and 3.17 in this document."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a team designs or reviews assessments, or a student asks about alternative arrangements. Frame it for staff as an expectation of inclusive design first and adjustments second. Ask: 'Which of your assessments would need the most adjustment, and could it be designed differently?' Avoid placing the duty on the student."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2
  forPractitioner: { text: "When deciding how to evidence achievement above threshold, use Annex D's descriptions of the 1st, 2.1, 2.2 and 3rd classes as the common reference.", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "not-applicable: a duty on the provider or course team; the student meets its effect, not a standard to reach", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a should-level expectation on providers to refer to Annex D (std:doc:qaa-fheq-2024-framework, Annex D) when judging achievement, with the statement that most students exceed threshold. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2. Do not claim the statement maps its rungs onto degree classes; set it beside 4.1 and the Annex D third-class descriptions such as qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/summary/r1/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a team sets marking criteria or links the rungs to classes. Frame it for staff as a pointer to Annex D, with the mapping theirs to make. Ask: 'How do your marking bands relate to the Annex D descriptions, and where did you decide that?' Avoid telling a student that the typical rung equals a particular class."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b1
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in agriculture will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the scientific principles and theories, agricultural, animal and environmental, on which sustainable production systems rest. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the scientific principles and theories, agricultural, animal and environmental, on which sustainable production systems rest.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in agriculture will have met: at least some familiarity with the science behind sustainable production (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b1.typical and 4.2.b1.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in agriculture asks what they need to know about the science behind sustainable production to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met the science behind sustainable production, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b1.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in agriculture will have a deep and thorough understanding of the scientific principles and theories, agricultural, animal and environmental, on which sustainable production systems rest; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of the scientific principles and theories, agricultural, animal and environmental, on which sustainable production systems rest.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of the science behind sustainable production for honours graduates in agriculture (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b1 and 4.2.b1.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in agriculture, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of the science behind sustainable production that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on the science behind sustainable production and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b1.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in agriculture will have a sound, well-founded understanding of the scientific principles and theories, agricultural, animal and environmental, on which sustainable production systems rest: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of the scientific principles and theories, agricultural, animal and environmental, on which sustainable production systems rest.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of the science behind sustainable production that most honours graduates in agriculture reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b1 and 4.2.b1.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of the science behind sustainable production wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does the science behind sustainable production involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b2
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in agriculture will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, how crops are grown sustainably and how animals are kept to high welfare standards. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with how crops are grown sustainably and how animals are kept to high welfare standards.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in agriculture will have met: at least some familiarity with sustainable crops and high-welfare husbandry (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b2.typical and 4.2.b2.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in agriculture asks what they need to know about sustainable crops and high-welfare husbandry to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met sustainable crops and high-welfare husbandry, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."

- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b3
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in agriculture will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the effects farming practice has on the agri-environment: landscape, soil, water, air, biodiversity and the climate. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the effects farming practice has on the agri-environment: landscape, soil, water, air, biodiversity and the climate.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in agriculture will have met: at least some familiarity with farming's environmental impact (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b3.typical and 4.2.b3.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in agriculture asks what they need to know about farming's environmental impact to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met farming's environmental impact, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."

- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b4
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in agriculture will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, how sustainable, ethical production systems are developed and run to meet a farm's aims, from economic viability and food security to social inclusion, animal health and welfare and environmental gain. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with how sustainable, ethical production systems are developed and run to meet a farm's aims, from economic viability and food security to social inclusion, animal health and welfare and environmental gain.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in agriculture will have met: at least some familiarity with sustainable, ethical production systems (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b4.typical and 4.2.b4.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."

- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b5
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in agriculture will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, how agri-tech is applied and built into farming, and how digital data can guide management decisions well. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with how agri-tech is applied and built into farming, and how digital data can guide management decisions well.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in agriculture will have met: at least some familiarity with agri-tech and digital data for decisions (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b5.typical and 4.2.b5.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in agriculture asks what they need to know about agri-tech and digital data for decisions to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b5.excellent

  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of agri-tech and digital data for decisions for honours graduates in agriculture (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland).   author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in agriculture, or with a team designing stretch tasks. - statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b5.typical

  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of how agri-tech is applied and built into farming, and how digital data can guide management decisions well.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of agri-tech and digital data for decisions that most honours graduates in agriculture reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, typical column.   author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of agri-tech and digital data for decisions wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module.   author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in agriculture asks what they need to know about farming, global change and net zero to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met farming, global change and net zero, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b10.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in agriculture will have a deep and thorough understanding of farming's part in global environmental change and the practical steps for managing carbon on farms on the way to net zero; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of farming's part in global environmental change and the practical steps for managing carbon on farms on the way to net zero.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of farming, global change and net zero for honours graduates in agriculture (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b10 and 4.2.b10.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in agriculture, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of farming, global change and net zero that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on farming, global change and net zero and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b10.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in agriculture will have a sound, well-founded understanding of farming's part in global environmental change and the practical steps for managing carbon on farms on the way to net zero: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of farming's part in global environmental change and the practical steps for managing carbon on farms on the way to net zero.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of farming, global change and net zero that most honours graduates in agriculture reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Agriculture table, typical column.   author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of farming, global change and net zero wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module.   forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in horticulture will have a deep and thorough understanding of the science behind propagation and plant manipulation, including breeding, genomic and phenomic methods and growth regulators; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of the science behind propagation and plant manipulation, including breeding, genomic and phenomic methods and growth regulators.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of propagation, breeding and plant manipulation for honours graduates in horticulture (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Horticulture table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b15 and 4.2.b15.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in horticulture, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of propagation, breeding and plant manipulation that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on propagation, breeding and plant manipulation and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b15.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in horticulture will have a sound, well-founded understanding of the science behind propagation and plant manipulation, including breeding, genomic and phenomic methods and growth regulators: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of the science behind propagation and plant manipulation, including breeding, genomic and phenomic methods and growth regulators.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of propagation, breeding and plant manipulation that most honours graduates in horticulture reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Horticulture table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b15 and 4.2.b15.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of propagation, breeding and plant manipulation wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does propagation, breeding and plant manipulation involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b16
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in horticulture will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, running plant production systems for both conservation and commercial ends across many kinds of plant. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with running plant production systems for both conservation and commercial ends across many kinds of plant.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in horticulture will have met: at least some familiarity with managing plant production systems (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Horticulture table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b16.typical and 4.2.b16.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in horticulture asks what they need to know about managing plant production systems to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met managing plant production systems, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in horticulture will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the science and technology on which growing edible and ornamental crops depends. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the science and technology on which growing edible and ornamental crops depends.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in horticulture will have met: at least some familiarity with the science and technology of crop production (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Horticulture table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b17.typical and 4.2.b17.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."

Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Horticulture table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b20 and 4.2.b20.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b2 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/practical/r1/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in horticulture, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of evaluating practice and recommending change that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on evaluating practice and recommending change and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b20.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in horticulture will have a sound, well-founded understanding of appraising current commercial horticultural practice and, where it fits, proposing changes towards a more sustainable future: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of appraising current commercial horticultural practice and, where it fits, proposing changes towards a more sustainable future.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of evaluating practice and recommending change that most honours graduates in horticulture reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Horticulture table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b20 and 4.2.b20.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b2 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/practical/r1/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/practical/r1/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of evaluating practice and recommending change wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does evaluating practice and recommending change involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b21
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in horticulture will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the policy, rules, law, ethics and health and safety guidance that apply to growing and manipulating plants, horticultural practice and landscape upkeep. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the policy, rules, law, ethics and health and safety guidance that apply to growing and manipulating plants, horticultural practice and landscape upkeep.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in horticulture will have met: at least some familiarity with horticultural policy, law and safety (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Horticulture table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b21.typical and 4.2.b21.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in horticulture asks what they need to know about horticultural policy, law and safety to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met horticultural policy, law and safety, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b21.excellent

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in horticulture will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, horticultural practice in the UK and worldwide, taking in gender equity, socio-economic conditions and public worries about sustainable land use and growing.   author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in horticulture will have met: at least some familiarity with horticulture at home and abroad (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Horticulture table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours.   forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in forestry will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the scientific and commercial grounds for managing trees and forests. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the scientific and commercial grounds for managing trees and forests.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in forestry will have met: at least some familiarity with the science and commerce of forest management (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Forestry table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b23.typical and 4.2.b23.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."

  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of the physical and ecological processes that shape where natural, semi-natural and managed forests occur and what they contain.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of processes shaping forests for honours graduates in forestry (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Forestry table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b25 and 4.2.b25.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in forestry, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of processes shaping forests that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on processes shaping forests and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b25.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in forestry will have a sound, well-founded understanding of the physical and ecological processes that shape where natural, semi-natural and managed forests occur and what they contain: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of the physical and ecological processes that shape where natural, semi-natural and managed forests occur and what they contain.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of processes shaping forests that most honours graduates in forestry reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Forestry table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b25 and 4.2.b25.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of processes shaping forests wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does processes shaping forests involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b26
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in forestry will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, what forests contribute through provisioning, supporting and regulating ecosystem services. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with what forests contribute through provisioning, supporting and regulating ecosystem services.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in forestry will have met: at least some familiarity with forest ecosystem services (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Forestry table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b26.typical and 4.2.b26.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in forestry asks what they need to know about forest ecosystem services to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met forest ecosystem services, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b26.excellent

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in forestry will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, why weighing social, economic and environmental aims together matters for managing forests sustainably.
