```yaml
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in forestry will have a sound, well-founded understanding of ways of making forests more resistant and resilient to climate change, pests and diseases: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of ways of making forests more resistant and resilient to climate change, pests and diseases.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of forest resilience that most honours graduates in forestry reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Forestry table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b30 and 4.2.b30.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of forest resilience wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does forest resilience involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b31
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in forestry will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the social, legal and policy setting for forestry in the UK and internationally. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the social, legal and policy setting for forestry in the UK and internationally.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in forestry will have met: at least some familiarity with forestry law and policy (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Forestry table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b31.typical and 4.2.b31.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in forestry asks what they need to know about forestry law and policy to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met forestry law and policy, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b31.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in forestry will have a deep and thorough understanding of the social, legal and policy setting for forestry in the UK and internationally; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of the social, legal and policy setting for forestry in the UK and internationally.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of forestry law and policy for honours graduates in forestry (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Forestry table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b31 and 4.2.b31.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in forestry, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of forestry law and policy that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on forestry law and policy and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in rural environmental sciences will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the physical and ecological features of the countryside and what constrains its development. Set it beside 4.2.b35 and 4.2.b35.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of principles behind rural industries wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does principles behind rural industries involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b36
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in rural environmental sciences will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the principles of conserving wildlife and landscape, seen in past, present and future contexts. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the principles of conserving wildlife and landscape, seen in past, present and future contexts.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in rural environmental sciences will have met: at least some familiarity with wildlife and landscape conservation (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Rural Environmental Sciences table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b36.typical and 4.2.b36.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in rural environmental sciences asks what they need to know about wildlife and landscape conservation to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met wildlife and landscape conservation, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b36.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in rural environmental sciences will have a deep and thorough understanding of the principles of conserving wildlife and landscape, seen in past, present and future contexts; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of the principles of conserving wildlife and landscape, seen in past, present and future contexts.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of wildlife and landscape conservation for honours graduates in rural environmental sciences (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Rural Environmental Sciences table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b36 and 4.2.b36.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in rural environmental sciences, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of wildlife and landscape conservation that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on wildlife and landscape conservation and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b36.typical

  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of the principles of conserving wildlife and landscape, seen in past, present and future contexts.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }

  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of wildlife and landscape conservation wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. Ask: 'What does conflicts and trade-offs in the countryside involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b41
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in rural environmental sciences will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, how rural businesses, communities and other parties can be helped to develop and look after the countryside sustainably. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with how rural businesses, communities and other parties can be helped to develop and look after the countryside sustainably.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in rural environmental sciences will have met: at least some familiarity with supporting sustainable rural development (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Rural Environmental Sciences table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b41.typical and 4.2.b41.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in rural environmental sciences asks what they need to know about supporting sustainable rural development to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met supporting sustainable rural development, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b41.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in rural environmental sciences will have a deep and thorough understanding of how rural businesses, communities and other parties can be helped to develop and look after the countryside sustainably; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of how rural businesses, communities and other parties can be helped to develop and look after the countryside sustainably.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of supporting sustainable rural development for honours graduates in rural environmental sciences (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Rural Environmental Sciences table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b41 and 4.2.b41.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in rural environmental sciences, or with a team designing stretch tasks. Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in rural environmental sciences will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, using GIS, remote sensing and other environmental science methods to manage, assess and monitor the countryside. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b42.typical and 4.2.b42.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, how animals' quality of life can be made as good as possible, using evidence-informed management and practice to secure positive welfare.   author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in animal studies (non-production animals) will have met: at least some familiarity with optimising animals' quality of life (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b43.typical and 4.2.b43.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."

To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does evolution, breeding, health, behaviour and nutrition involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b46
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, how sustainable animal management and practice is, financially, environmentally and socially, at global, national and local scales. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with how sustainable animal management and practice is, financially, environmentally and socially, at global, national and local scales.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in animal studies (non-production animals) will have met: at least some familiarity with the sustainability of animal management (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Animal Studies table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b46.typical and 4.2.b46.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in animal studies (non-production animals) asks what they need to know about the sustainability of animal management to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met the sustainability of animal management, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b46.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) will have a deep and thorough understanding of how sustainable animal management and practice is, financially, environmentally and socially, at global, national and local scales; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of how sustainable animal management and practice is, financially, environmentally and socially, at global, national and local scales.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of the sustainability of animal management for honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Animal Studies table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b46 and 4.2.b46.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in animal studies (non-production animals), or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of the sustainability of animal management that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on the sustainability of animal management and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b46.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) will have a sound, well-founded understanding of how sustainable animal management and practice is, financially, environmentally and socially, at global, national and local scales: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of how sustainable animal management and practice is, financially, environmentally and socially, at global, national and local scales.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of the sustainability of animal management that most honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Animal Studies table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b46 and 4.2.b46.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of the sustainability of animal management wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does the sustainability of animal management involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."

  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of industry-led and industry-leading practice wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. - statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b51
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the part education and communication play in changing human behaviour so that animals are managed as well as possible. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the part education and communication play in changing human behaviour so that animals are managed as well as possible.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in animal studies (non-production animals) will have met: at least some familiarity with education for human behaviour change (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Animal Studies table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b51.typical and 4.2.b51.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in animal studies (non-production animals) asks what they need to know about education for human behaviour change to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met education for human behaviour change, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b51.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) will have a deep and thorough understanding of the part education and communication play in changing human behaviour so that animals are managed as well as possible; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of the part education and communication play in changing human behaviour so that animals are managed as well as possible.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of education for human behaviour change for honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Animal Studies table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b51 and 4.2.b51.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in animal studies (non-production animals), or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of education for human behaviour change that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on education for human behaviour change and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."

- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b52
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in animal studies (non-production animals) will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, why interactions between people and animals matter to society, and the need to weigh animal welfare in every interaction, such as in sport, therapy or education. - statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b53
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, food science and the chemistry and make-up of food, nutrients and non-nutrients alike, with the physiology, biochemistry, genetics and nutrition that form the basis for seeing how food affects human health. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with food science and the chemistry and make-up of food, nutrients and non-nutrients alike, with the physiology, biochemistry, genetics and nutrition that form the basis for seeing how food affects human health.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with food science and human health (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b53.typical and 4.2.b53.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in food, nutrition and consumer science asks what they need to know about food science and human health to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. - statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b55.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a sound, well-founded understanding of physical, chemical, microbiological and allergen analysis of food: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of physical, chemical, microbiological and allergen analysis of food.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of food analysis that most honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b55 and 4.2.b55.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of food analysis wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does food analysis involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b56
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, why the sensory side of food, nutrition and health matters for consumer choice, and how sensory evaluation is used to measure product attributes and consumer response. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with why the sensory side of food, nutrition and health matters for consumer choice, and how sensory evaluation is used to measure product attributes and consumer response.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with sensory science (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b56.typical and 4.2.b56.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in food, nutrition and consumer science asks what they need to know about sensory science to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met sensory science, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b56.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a deep and thorough understanding of why the sensory side of food, nutrition and health matters for consumer choice, and how sensory evaluation is used to measure product attributes and consumer response; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of why the sensory side of food, nutrition and health matters for consumer choice, and how sensory evaluation is used to measure product attributes and consumer response.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of sensory science for honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b56 and 4.2.b56.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in food, nutrition and consumer science, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of sensory science that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on sensory science and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b56.typical

- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b57
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, why quality control and assurance, food safety and systems for managing waste and the environment matter to the food industry. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with why quality control and assurance, food safety and systems for managing waste and the environment matter to the food industry.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with quality, safety and management systems (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b57.typical and 4.2.b57.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in food, nutrition and consumer science asks what they need to know about quality, safety and management systems to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met quality, safety and management systems, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."

- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b58
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the principles and practice of preserving and processing food, and their effects on its safety and nutritional quality. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the principles and practice of preserving and processing food, and their effects on its safety and nutritional quality.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with preservation and processing (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b58.typical and 4.2.b58.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the main engineering and innovation points in designing food equipment and premises hygienically. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the main engineering and innovation points in designing food equipment and premises hygienically.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with hygienic design (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b59.typical and 4.2.b59.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."

- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b60.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a sound, well-founded understanding of raising food productivity, nutritional quality and efficiency with new digital technologies: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of raising food productivity, nutritional quality and efficiency with new digital technologies.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of digital innovation in food production that most honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b60 and 4.2.b60.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b2 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/practical/r1/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/practical/r1/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of digital innovation in food production wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does digital innovation in food production involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b61
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the standard methods for detecting and counting microorganisms that matter for food safety. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the standard methods for detecting and counting microorganisms that matter for food safety.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with food microbiology methods (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b61.typical and 4.2.b61.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in food, nutrition and consumer science asks what they need to know about food microbiology methods to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met food microbiology methods, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b61.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a deep and thorough understanding of the standard methods for detecting and counting microorganisms that matter for food safety; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of the standard methods for detecting and counting microorganisms that matter for food safety.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of food microbiology methods for honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b61 and 4.2.b61.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in food, nutrition and consumer science, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of food microbiology methods that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on food microbiology methods and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."

- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b62
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the risks from food fraud, intolerances and allergies, and why food risk management and new traceability methods are needed to keep food safe and acceptable to consumers. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }

  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with food risk and traceability (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland).   forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, applying knowledge and conveying it to varied audiences in fitting formats and media, to serve consumers, industry and society in securing a food supply that is sustainable, ethical, safe, affordable, assured and resilient.   forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with applying knowledge and conveying it to varied audiences in fitting formats and media, to serve consumers, industry and society in securing a food supply that is sustainable, ethical, safe, affordable, assured and resilient.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }

Set it beside 4.2.b65 and 4.2.b65.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in food, nutrition and consumer science, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of global food system challenges that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on global food system challenges and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b65.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a sound, well-founded understanding of the global challenges of producing, processing and making food commodities ethically and sustainably and of supplying consumer services, with the approaches that make supply chains resilient and reduce their impact: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of the global challenges of producing, processing and making food commodities ethically and sustainably and of supplying consumer services, with the approaches that make supply chains resilient and reduce their impact.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of global food system challenges that most honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b65 and 4.2.b65.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of global food system challenges wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does global food system challenges involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b66
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, designing or reformulating a diet or food product for the needs of a person or group, taking account of age, gender, religion, culture, socio-economic background, place, environment, physical activity and sustainability. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with designing or reformulating a diet or food product for the needs of a person or group, taking account of age, gender, religion, culture, socio-economic background, place, environment, physical activity and sustainability.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with designing diets and products for particular needs (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b66.typical and 4.2.b66.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b2 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/practical/r1/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in food, nutrition and consumer science asks what they need to know about designing diets and products for particular needs to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met designing diets and products for particular needs, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b66.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a deep and thorough understanding of designing or reformulating a diet or food product for the needs of a person or group, taking account of age, gender, religion, culture, socio-economic background, place, environment, physical activity and sustainability; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, food, nutrition, health and consumer policy at local, national and international levels.   forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a deep and thorough understanding of the legal, moral and ethical issues and frameworks, professional codes of conduct among them, that bear on the food, nutrition and consumer sectors; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of the legal, moral and ethical issues and frameworks, professional codes of conduct among them, that bear on the food, nutrition and consumer sectors.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of law, ethics and codes of conduct for honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b70 and 4.2.b70.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in food, nutrition and consumer science, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of law, ethics and codes of conduct that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on law, ethics and codes of conduct and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b70.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a sound, well-founded understanding of the legal, moral and ethical issues and frameworks, professional codes of conduct among them, that bear on the food, nutrition and consumer sectors: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of the legal, moral and ethical issues and frameworks, professional codes of conduct among them, that bear on the food, nutrition and consumer sectors.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of law, ethics and codes of conduct that most honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b70 and 4.2.b70.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of law, ethics and codes of conduct wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does law, ethics and codes of conduct involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b71
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the business setting of agri-food firms and why innovation and entrepreneurship matter in developing food that is affordable, sustainable, nutritious and safe. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with the business setting of agri-food firms and why innovation and entrepreneurship matter in developing food that is affordable, sustainable, nutritious and safe.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with the agri-food business environment (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b71.typical and 4.2.b71.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in food, nutrition and consumer science asks what they need to know about the agri-food business environment to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met the agri-food business environment, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, connected digital manufacturing systems for communication and analysis, and using big data to spot trends, improve decisions and drive change that cuts waste, meets food security challenges and responds to shifting consumer needs. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }

  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with connected manufacturing and big data (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b72.typical and 4.2.b72.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."

  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, the social and economic reasons access to goods and services differs, and the inequity various consumer groups meet in that access.   forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, what consumer organisations, food and health charities, nutrition bodies, public health agencies and nutrition and health professionals do and are responsible for. Teaching and assessment need to secure this for every graduate.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "To reach the threshold, you will have at least some familiarity with what consumer organisations, food and health charities, nutrition bodies, public health agencies and nutrition and health professionals do and are responsible for.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a threshold outcome that every honours graduate in food, nutrition and consumer science will have met: at least some familiarity with roles of consumer and health bodies (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, threshold column. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a mark scheme or a standard for any award other than honours. Set it beside the same outcome at 4.2.b75.typical and 4.2.b75.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and the Annex D third-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/3rd."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student in food, nutrition and consumer science asks what they need to know about roles of consumer and health bodies to pass, or when a team checks that every graduate meets the minimum. To a student, say plainly that by the end you need at least a working familiarity with it; to staff, frame it as the floor the course has to secure. Ask: 'Where in your course have you met roles of consumer and health bodies, and could you explain the basics in your own words?' Do not read the table aloud, and do not present the typical or excellent columns as the minimum."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b75.excellent
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the excellent rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a deep and thorough understanding of what consumer organisations, food and health charities, nutrition bodies, public health agencies and nutrition and health professionals do and are responsible for; design tasks that give the strongest students room to show it.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the excellent level, you will have a deep and thorough understanding of what consumer organisations, food and health charities, nutrition bodies, public health agencies and nutrition and health professionals do and are responsible for.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the excellent rung, an aspiration rather than a requirement: a deep, thorough understanding of roles of consumer and health bodies for honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, excellent column. Do not present it as expected of every graduate, as regulation or as master's-level work. Set it beside 4.2.b75 and 4.2.b75.typical in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D first-class description qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/1st."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this with a student aiming for the top of the range in food, nutrition and consumer science, or with a team designing stretch tasks. To a student, describe it as a deep, thorough command of roles of consumer and health bodies that you can apply to new cases; to staff, as what the strongest work shows. Ask: 'Could you weigh competing views on roles of consumer and health bodies and apply them to a case you have not met before?' Avoid implying that everyone has to reach this or that falling short of it means failing."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b75.typical
  forPractitioner: { text: "At the typical rung, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have a sound, well-founded understanding of what consumer organisations, food and health charities, nutrition bodies, public health agencies and nutrition and health professionals do and are responsible for: the level most of them reach.", rewrite: light, force: will, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "At the typical level, you will have a sound understanding of what consumer organisations, food and health charities, nutrition bodies, public health agencies and nutrition and health professionals do and are responsible for.", rewrite: full, force: will, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as the typical rung: a well-founded understanding of roles of consumer and health bodies that most honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science reach, above the threshold (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland). Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.2, Food Science, Technology, Nutrition and Consumer Science table, typical column. Do not claim it as the pass standard or as regulation, and do not equate it with one degree class: the statement draws no such line. Set it beside 4.2.b75 and 4.2.b75.excellent in this document, the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b1 and, as an inferred reading only, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.2 and qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/knowledge/r2/2.1."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Raise this when a student who has the basics of roles of consumer and health bodies wants to know what a solid result looks like, or when a team plans the core of a module. To a student, put it as the sound understanding most people reach by the end; to staff, as the expected standard above the floor. Ask: 'What does roles of consumer and health bodies involve in practice, and why does it matter?' Avoid presenting it as the pass mark or reading the issuer's wording at length."
- statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.2.b76
  forPractitioner: { text: "At threshold, honours graduates in food, nutrition and consumer science will have at least some familiarity with, and awareness of, theories and methods for understanding how consumers respond, choose and behave, and for designing and running consumer and intervention studies. - statement: qaa/agriculture-rural-environmental-sciences-animal-studies-consumer-science-forestry-food-horticulture-and-human-nutrition-2024/4.3.b1
  forPractitioner: { text: "On graduating with honours, students should be able to show that they can listen to new ideas or information and take them in (people skills).", rewrite: light, force: should, status: drafted }
  forBeneficiary: { text: "By the time you graduate, you should be able to show that you can listen to new ideas or information and take them in.", rewrite: full, force: should, status: drafted }
  author-agent-written: "Treat this as a generic skill that honours graduates in these subjects should be able to show at graduation (FHEQ level 6; FQHEIS level 10 in Scotland), listed under People skills outside the three rungs. Cite it as QAA (2024) Agriculture ... Human Nutrition Subject Benchmark Statement, para 4.3. Do not present it as a regulatory requirement, a rung of the subject tables or a skill pitched at another level. Set it beside the FHEQ honours descriptor qaa/fheq-2024/4.15.b8, the Annex D descriptions qaa/fheq-2024/annex-d/transferable/r1/3rd to 1st and the reference step spine:6."
  author-agent-dialogue: "Bring this up when a student or team talks about listening to new ideas, in study, placements or careers planning. To a student, put it in plain words as something you should be able to do by graduation; to staff, as a generic outcome to build and assess across the course. Ask: 'When did you last meet an unfamiliar idea in class, and how did you check you had grasped it?' Avoid reading the issuer's list aloud or treating it as one of the subject-specific rungs."
```

### S9 Commentary (optional)
<!-- label: editorial; status: drafted; basis: secondary -->
`unknown`: not researched in P1 (Part E).

### SB1 The subject
<!-- label: both; status: drafted; basis: primary -->
- A family of separate but closely related land-based and food subjects, each a subject in its own right, running from field to fork (D1, front matter; 1.1, 1.2).
- Its context is economic and social: the subjects bear on global sustainability, and graduates make evidence-informed decisions from international policy down to local practice (D1, 1.4).
- Sustainability is a priority, taken holistically and through approaches such as One Health that link people, animals, plants and environment (D1, 1.13, 1.14).
- Agriculture covers farming systems, crop and animal production, natural resources and farm economics; horticulture covers plant science, production systems and landscapes; forestry covers woodland and forest management for market and wider benefits (D1, 2.4 to 2.6).
- Rural environmental sciences treat the countryside as a complex environmental and cultural resource, multidisciplinary and analytical (D1, 2.7).
- Animal studies concern the husbandry, welfare and management of non-production animals and a good life for them (D1, 2.8; 3.5; 4.2).
- Food science, food technology, human nutrition and consumer science span the food supply chain, safe and sustainable food, health and how consumer choices are made and changed (D1, 2.9, 2.10).
- The subjects are practical: field observation, empirical data, laboratory and field work and work-based learning are central (D1, 3.12, 3.17).

### SB3 Content and delivery
<!-- label: both; status: drafted; basis: primary -->
```yaml
areas:
  - { component: "3.1", gloss: "agriculture (production, sustainable management and agri-tech, farm business); horticulture (plant biology, crop systems, landscapes); forestry (forest and woodland management, conservation)" }
  - { component: "3.3", gloss: "the rural environment, managed and unmanaged landscapes, land management" }
  - { component: "3.5", gloss: "applied animal science and management (equine, canine, zoo) and wildlife conservation" }
  - { component: "3.7", gloss: "food composition and quality, processing and packaging, safety and fraud, diet and health, nutrition science, ethics, consumer behaviour and research methods" }
practices:
  - { component: "3.9", kind: "teaching", gloss: "active, individualised, differentiated approaches; students as partners" }
  - { component: "3.10", kind: "teaching", gloss: "blended and online learning, with care for the digital divide" }
  - { component: "3.12", kind: "learning", gloss: "work-based learning, placements and apprenticeships, made equitable with partners" }
  - { component: "3.16", kind: "teaching", gloss: "lectures, practicals in real settings, seminars, group work, case studies, problem-based learning, self-study, visits, placements, a project or dissertation" }
  - { component: "3.17", kind: "learning", gloss: "field observation, practical and research skills, inclusive fieldwork" }
  - { component: "3.18", kind: "assessment", gloss: "a varied strategy with choice, peer and self-assessment" }
  - { component: "3.20", kind: "assessment", gloss: "formative and summative, continuous and controlled; authentic tasks" }
  - { component: "3.21", kind: "assessment", gloss: "inclusive assessment with reasonable adjustments" }
```

### B1 Concepts
<!-- label: both; status: drafted; basis: primary -->
```yaml
- { slug: "hidden-curriculum", definition: "the implicit lessons students absorb beyond formal teaching, which shape their sense of the discipline's values and norms and can be a barrier when access to them is unequal (D1, 1.10)", beneficiaryDefinition: "the unwritten things you pick up about how your subject and course work, beyond what is taught", scopeNote: "used of course design and EDI", category: "unknown", practices: ["education"] }
- { slug: "accessibility", definition: "inclusive design of curriculum, teaching and the wider student experience so that nobody is shut out, above all through barriers linked to disability (D1, 1.11)", beneficiaryDefinition: "courses designed so that everyone, including disabled students, can take part", scopeNote: "a cross-cutting theme", category: "unknown", practices: ["education"] }
- { slug: "education-for-sustainable-development", definition: "after UNESCO (2019), holistic and transformational education covering content, outcomes, pedagogy and environment, aiming to change society (D1, 1.14)", beneficiaryDefinition: "learning that helps you understand and act on sustainability, and changes how you see the world", scopeNote: "ESD; a cross-cutting theme", category: "unknown", practices: ["education"] }
- { slug: "sustainability", definition: "a continuing process of tackling social, environmental, ethical, political and economic concerns for a better world, not only an environmental matter (D1, 1.13)", beneficiaryDefinition: "meeting today's needs fairly while protecting the planet and people for the future", scopeNote: "used with sustainable development", category: "unknown", practices: ["education", "agriculture"] }
- { slug: "one-health", definition: "an approach that sees people, animals, plants and their shared environment as interconnected (D1, 1.13)", beneficiaryDefinition: "the idea that human, animal and plant health and the environment depend on each other", scopeNote: "named as an example", category: "unknown", practices: ["agriculture", "health"] }
- { slug: "work-based-learning", definition: "learning through placements, internships and apprenticeships (D1, 3.12)", beneficiaryDefinition: "learning you do in a workplace, such as a placement or apprenticeship", scopeNote: "WBL", category: "unknown", practices: ["education"] }
- { slug: "authentic-assessment", definition: "assessment built on tasks of the kind met in the workplace (D1, 3.20)", beneficiaryDefinition: "assessment that asks you to do the kind of task you would do at work", scopeNote: "examples in 3.23", category: "unknown", practices: ["education"] }
```
